		<li class="gallerybox" style="width: 155px">
			<div class="thumb" style="width: 150px; height: 150px;"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Archand%C4%9Bl_Gabriel_z_Nedv%C4%9Bdic,_AJG_Hlubok%C3%A1_nad_Vltavou.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="Archangel Gabriel of Nedvědice"><img alt="Archangel Gabriel of Nedvědice" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Archand%C4%9Bl_Gabriel_z_Nedv%C4%9Bdic%2C_AJG_Hlubok%C3%A1_nad_Vltavou.jpg/84px-Archand%C4%9Bl_Gabriel_z_Nedv%C4%9Bdic%2C_AJG_Hlubok%C3%A1_nad_Vltavou.jpg" decoding="async" width="84" height="120" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Archand%C4%9Bl_Gabriel_z_Nedv%C4%9Bdic%2C_AJG_Hlubok%C3%A1_nad_Vltavou.jpg/127px-Archand%C4%9Bl_Gabriel_z_Nedv%C4%9Bdic%2C_AJG_Hlubok%C3%A1_nad_Vltavou.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Archand%C4%9Bl_Gabriel_z_Nedv%C4%9Bdic%2C_AJG_Hlubok%C3%A1_nad_Vltavou.jpg/169px-Archand%C4%9Bl_Gabriel_z_Nedv%C4%9Bdic%2C_AJG_Hlubok%C3%A1_nad_Vltavou.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1128" data-file-height="1600" /></a></span></div>
			<div class="gallerytext">
<p><a href="/wiki/Archangel_Gabriel_of_Nedv%C4%9Bdice" title="Archangel Gabriel of Nedvědice">Archangel Gabriel of Nedvědice</a>
</p>
			</div>
		</li>
</ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Festivals">Festivals</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabriel&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Festivals">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>Baltimore's "<a href="/wiki/Little_Italy,_Baltimore" title="Little Italy, Baltimore">Little Italy</a>" has for over 80 years hosted an annual "end of summer" St. Gabriel Festival that features a procession with a statue of the saint carried through the streets.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup></li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Film">Film</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabriel&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Film">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>In <i><a href="/wiki/Liliom_(1930_film)" title="Liliom (1930 film)">Liliom</a></i> (1930), Gabriel is portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Harvey_Clark_(actor)" title="Harvey Clark (actor)">Harvey Clark</a>.</li>
<li>In <i><a href="/wiki/The_Green_Pastures_(film)" title="The Green Pastures (film)">The Green Pastures</a></i> (1936), Gabriel is portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Oscar_Polk" title="Oscar Polk">Oscar Polk</a>.</li>
<li>In <i><a href="/wiki/Heaven_Only_Knows_(1947_film)" class="mw-redirect" title="Heaven Only Knows (1947 film)">Heaven Only Knows</a></i> (1947), Gabriel was portrayed by <a href="/wiki/William_Farnum" title="William Farnum">William Farnum</a>.</li>
<li>In <i>The Littlest Angel</i> (1969; television film), Gabriel is portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Cab_Calloway" title="Cab Calloway">Cab Calloway</a>.</li>
<li>In horror film <i><a href="/wiki/The_Prophecy" title="The Prophecy">The Prophecy</a></i> (1995), Gabriel (portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Walken" title="Christopher Walken">Christopher Walken</a>) searches for an evil soul on Earth during an <a href="/wiki/Apocalypse" title="Apocalypse">end-of-days</a> angelic civil war. He is also a character in <i><a href="/wiki/The_Prophecy_II" title="The Prophecy II">The Prophecy II</a></i> (1998) and <i><a href="/wiki/The_Prophecy_3:_The_Ascent" title="The Prophecy 3: The Ascent">The Prophecy 3: The Ascent</a></i> (2000).</li>
<li>In <i><a href="/wiki/Mary,_Mother_of_Jesus_(film)" title="Mary, Mother of Jesus (film)">Mary, Mother of Jesus</a></i> (1999; television film), Gabriel is portrayed by <a href="/wiki/John_Light_(actor)" title="John Light (actor)">John Light</a>.</li>
<li>In the fantasy/horror film <i><a href="/wiki/Van_Helsing_(film)" title="Van Helsing (film)">Van Helsing</a></i> (2004), the main character Van Helsing is alluded to be Gabriel, portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Hugh_Jackman" title="Hugh Jackman">Hugh Jackman</a>.</li>
<li>In the fantasy/horror film <i><a href="/wiki/Constantine_(film)" title="Constantine (film)">Constantine</a></i> (2005), <a href="/wiki/Tilda_Swinton" title="Tilda Swinton">Tilda Swinton</a> portrays an androgynous archangel Gabriel.</li>
<li>In the action/horror film <i><a href="/wiki/Gabriel_(2007_film)" title="Gabriel (2007 film)">Gabriel</a></i> (2007), the eponymous character (portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Andy_Whitfield" title="Andy Whitfield">Andy Whitfield</a>) fights to save the <a href="/wiki/Soul" title="Soul">souls</a> in <a href="/wiki/Purgatory" title="Purgatory">purgatory</a> by defeating the evil <a href="/wiki/Fallen_angel" title="Fallen angel">fallen angels</a>.</li>
<li>In the apocalyptic supernatural action film <i><a href="/wiki/Legion_(2010_film)" title="Legion (2010 film)">Legion</a></i> (2010), <a href="/wiki/Kevin_Durand" title="Kevin Durand">Kevin Durand</a> plays the role of Archangel Gabriel, the leader of the angel army, and the main antagonist. The story was continued in the TV series <i><a href="/wiki/Dominion_(TV_series)" title="Dominion (TV series)">Dominion</a></i>.</li>
<li>In the analog horror series <i><a href="/wiki/The_Mandela_Catalogue" title="The Mandela Catalogue">The Mandela Catalogue</a></i>, A video titled "Overthrone" portrays Gabriel as an archangel tricking the shepherds into thinking that he was the true savior, not Jesus. This leads to the events of the series having hostile organisms called Alternates.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Games">Games</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabriel&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Games">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>2005: Spanish <a href="/wiki/Role-playing_game" title="Role-playing game">role-playing game</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Anima:_Beyond_Fantasy" class="mw-redirect" title="Anima: Beyond Fantasy">Anima: Beyond Fantasy</a></i> - Gabriel is as the humans know one of the seven "Beryls" (godlike beings of light) and is identified with the archangel of the same name. She has associated love, friendship, arts, and peace.</li>
<li>In the Japanese <a href="/wiki/Role-playing_game" title="Role-playing game">role-playing game</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Shin_Megami_Tensei" class="mw-redirect" title="Shin Megami Tensei">Shin Megami Tensei</a></i> - Gabriel is one of the Demons the player can <a href="/wiki/Summon" class="mw-redirect" title="Summon">summon</a> to assist in battle.</li>
<li>In the video game <i><a href="/wiki/El_Shaddai:_Ascension_of_the_Metatron" title="El Shaddai: Ascension of the Metatron">El Shaddai: Ascension of the Metatron</a></i>, based on the Book of Enoch, Gabriel is featured alongside Michael, Raphael, and Uriel as a guide for Enoch on his quest. All four archangels take the form of swans while on Earth. Gabriel is depicted as female in this interpretation and implied to be an angel of wisdom. She is associated with the Veil weapon Enoch uses.</li>
<li>Gabriel appears in the retro first-person shooter <i><a href="/wiki/Ultrakill" title="Ultrakill">ULTRAKILL</a></i>, who is voiced by Gianni Matragrano. He is featured as the final boss of the first two acts and a primary story character.</li>
<li>In <i><a href="/wiki/The_Binding_of_Isaac_(video_game)" title="The Binding of Isaac (video game)">The Binding of Isaac (video game)</a></i>, a roguelike dungeon crawler, the player is able to fight Gabriel and Uriel to obtain their key pieces in order to fight Mega Satan.</li>
<li>In <i><a href="/w/index.php?title=In_Death:_Unchained_(video_game)&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="In Death: Unchained (video game) (page does not exist)">In Death: Unchained (video game)</a></i>, a VR rougelike archery game, God has abandoned the Heaven and Gabriel lost his sanity. He is a boss of Paradise Lost area.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Literature">Literature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabriel&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Literature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>In his <a href="/wiki/Epic_poetry" title="Epic poetry">epic poem</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Paradise_Lost" title="Paradise Lost">Paradise Lost</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/John_Milton" title="John Milton">John Milton</a> made Gabriel chief of the angelic guards placed over <a href="/wiki/Garden_of_Eden" title="Garden of Eden">Paradise</a></li>
<li>The <a href="/wiki/Hebrew" class="mw-redirect" title="Hebrew">Hebrew</a> poem "Elifelet" (אליפלט) by <a href="/wiki/Nathan_Alterman" title="Nathan Alterman">Nathan Alterman</a>, put to music and often heard on the <a href="/wiki/Kol_Yisrael" title="Kol Yisrael">Israeli Radio</a>, tells of a heroic, self-sacrificing Israeli soldier being killed in battle. Upon the protagonist's death, the angel Gabriel descends to Earth, in order to comfort the spirit of the fallen hero and take him up to Heaven<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li>The main character of <a href="/wiki/Salman_Rushdie" title="Salman Rushdie">Salman Rushdie</a>'s <i><a href="/wiki/The_Satanic_Verses" title="The Satanic Verses">The Satanic Verses</a></i> (1988) believes that he is the modern incarnation of Gabriel.</li>
<li>In the Japanese <a href="/wiki/Light_novel" title="Light novel">light novel</a> series <i><a href="/wiki/No_Game_No_Life" title="No Game No Life">No Game No Life</a></i> (2012), Jibril is a member of the Flügel race and was a member of the Council of 18 Wings, a prominent section in the government. She is depicted as loving knowledge and books.</li>
<li>In volume 3 of the Japanese light novel series <i><a href="/wiki/The_Devil_Is_a_Part-Timer!" title="The Devil Is a Part-Timer!">The Devil Is a Part-Timer!</a></i>, an archangel named Gabriel appears and is the guardian of the <a href="/wiki/Sephirah" class="mw-redirect" title="Sephirah">Sephirah</a> <a href="/wiki/Yesod" title="Yesod">Yesod</a>.</li>
<li>In the Japanese light novel <i><a href="/wiki/High_School_DxD" title="High School DxD">High School DxD</a></i> features Gabriel as one of the Four Great Seraph whom are the highest ranking <a href="/wiki/Seraph" title="Seraph">Seraph</a> alongside Michael, Uriel and Raphael. In the novel, Gabriel is depicted as a female angel with immense angelic beauty and is given the titles of "The Strongest Woman in Heaven" and "The Most Beautiful Woman in Heaven".</li>
<li>In <a href="/wiki/August_Wilson" title="August Wilson">August Wilson</a>'s <i><a href="/wiki/Fences_(play)" title="Fences (play)">Fences</a></i> (1985), the mentally handicapped character Gabriel believes with every fiber of his soul that he is the Archangel Gabriel. He carries around a trumpet on him always, and strives to chase away the "hellhounds". In the last scene of the play, he calls for <a href="/wiki/Saint_Peter" title="Saint Peter">Saint Peter</a> to open up the gates.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabriel&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li>The eccentric English <a href="/wiki/Hagiography" title="Hagiography">hagiographer</a> and <a href="/wiki/Antiquarian" title="Antiquarian">antiquarian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sabine_Baring-Gould" title="Sabine Baring-Gould">Sabine Baring-Gould</a> (1834–1924) wrote "<a href="/wiki/Gabriel%27s_Message" title="Gabriel&#39;s Message">Gabriel's Message</a>", the English translation of the <a href="/wiki/Basque_music" title="Basque music">Basque</a> <a href="/wiki/Christmas" title="Christmas">Christmas</a> carol <i>Birjina gaztetto bat zegoen</i>. The original charol is likely related to the 13th or 14th-century Latin chant <i><a href="/wiki/Angelus_ad_virginem" title="Angelus ad virginem">Angelus Ad Virginem</a></i>, which itself is based on the biblical account of the <a href="/wiki/Annunciation" title="Annunciation">Annunciation</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_Luke" title="Gospel of Luke">Gospel of Luke</a>.</li>
<li>In "<a href="/wiki/My_Own_Prison" title="My Own Prison">My Own Prison</a>" by <a href="/wiki/Creed_(band)" title="Creed (band)">Creed</a>, Gabriel is mentioned as deciphering the visions to the main character in the song.</li>
<li>"<a href="/wiki/Sugar_Baby_(Bob_Dylan_song)" class="mw-redirect" title="Sugar Baby (Bob Dylan song)">Sugar Baby</a>", the last track on <a href="/wiki/Bob_Dylan" title="Bob Dylan">Bob Dylan</a>'s <i><a href="/wiki/Love_and_Theft_(Bob_Dylan_album)" title="Love and Theft (Bob Dylan album)">Love and Theft</a></i> album, contains this reference:</li></ul>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r996844942"><blockquote class="templatequote"><div class="poem">
<p>"Just as sure as we're living, just as sure as we're born<br />
Look up, look up - seek your Maker - 'fore Gabriel blows his horn.
</p>
</div></blockquote>
<ul><li>In the end of the song "Je N'en Connais Pas la Fin" by american <a href="/wiki/Post_metal" class="mw-redirect" title="Post metal">post metal</a> band <a href="/wiki/Rosetta" title="Rosetta">Rosetta</a> part of the lyrics said "Won't you play your trumpet well Gabriele", alluding to the announcement of apocalypse made by Gabriel in the bible.</li></ul>
<ul><li>"Blow Your Trumpets Gabriel" by Polish <a href="/wiki/Black_metal" title="Black metal">black metal</a> band <a href="/wiki/Behemoth_(band)" title="Behemoth (band)">Behemoth</a>.</li>
<li>The 1996 garage/house song "Gabriel" by <a href="/wiki/Roy_Davis_Jr." title="Roy Davis Jr.">Roy Davis Jr.</a> (featuring vocals from Peven Everett) is about the archangel Gabriel. In the chorus, Everett can be heard singing "Gabriel play" in reference to Gabriel's trumpet. A trumpet is also heard in the song right after this line is sung.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Visual_art">Visual art</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabriel&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Visual art">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<dl><dd><i>See also <a href="https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/Gabriel" class="extiw" title="commons:Gabriel">Gabriel gallery in Commons</a></i>.</dd></dl>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Leonardo_da_Vinci_Annunciazione_(dettaglio).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Leonardo_da_Vinci_Annunciazione_%28dettaglio%29.jpg/220px-Leonardo_da_Vinci_Annunciazione_%28dettaglio%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Leonardo_da_Vinci_Annunciazione_%28dettaglio%29.jpg/330px-Leonardo_da_Vinci_Annunciazione_%28dettaglio%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/13/Leonardo_da_Vinci_Annunciazione_%28dettaglio%29.jpg/440px-Leonardo_da_Vinci_Annunciazione_%28dettaglio%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5472" data-file-height="3648" /></a><figcaption>Detail of Gabriel from Leonardo da Vinci's <i><a href="/wiki/Annunciation_(Leonardo)" title="Annunciation (Leonardo)">Annunciation</a></i> (c. 1472–1475)</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Titian_-_Polyptych_of_the_Resurrection_-_Archangel_Gabriel_-_WGA22785.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Titian_-_Polyptych_of_the_Resurrection_-_Archangel_Gabriel_-_WGA22785.jpg/220px-Titian_-_Polyptych_of_the_Resurrection_-_Archangel_Gabriel_-_WGA22785.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="261" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Titian_-_Polyptych_of_the_Resurrection_-_Archangel_Gabriel_-_WGA22785.jpg/330px-Titian_-_Polyptych_of_the_Resurrection_-_Archangel_Gabriel_-_WGA22785.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Titian_-_Polyptych_of_the_Resurrection_-_Archangel_Gabriel_-_WGA22785.jpg/440px-Titian_-_Polyptych_of_the_Resurrection_-_Archangel_Gabriel_-_WGA22785.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1047" data-file-height="1241" /></a><figcaption>Angel of the <a href="/wiki/Averoldi_Polyptych#Annunciation" title="Averoldi Polyptych">Annunciation</a> by <a href="/wiki/Titian" title="Titian">Titian</a> (1520–1522)</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Daniel_8" title="Daniel 8">Daniel 8</a>:15 describes Gabriel as appearing in the "likeness of man" and in <a href="/wiki/Daniel_9" class="mw-redirect" title="Daniel 9">Daniel 9</a>:21 he is referred to as "the man Gabriel". David Everson observes that "such anthropomorphic descriptions of an angel are consistent with previous...descriptions of angels," as in <a href="/wiki/Genesis_19" class="mw-redirect" title="Genesis 19">Genesis 19</a>:5.<sup id="cite_ref-everson_15-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-everson-15">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Gabriel is most often portrayed in the context of scenes of the <a href="/wiki/Annunciation" title="Annunciation">Annunciation</a>. In 2008 a 16th-century drawing by Lucas van Leyden of the Netherlands was discovered. George R. Goldner, chairman of the department of prints and drawings at New York's <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_Museum_of_Art" title="Metropolitan Museum of Art">Metropolitan Museum of Art</a>, suggested that the sketch was for a stained glass window. "The fact that the archangel is an ordinary-looking person and not an idealized boy is typical of the artist", said Goldner.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In chronological order (to see each item, follow the link in the footnote):<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li><i>Archangel Gabriel</i> (<a href="/wiki/Triptych" title="Triptych">Triptych</a>), early 10th century, <a href="/wiki/Benaki_Museum" title="Benaki Museum">Benaki Museum</a></li>
<li><i>The Archangel Gabriel</i>, <a href="/wiki/Pisan" class="mw-redirect" title="Pisan">Pisan</a>, c. 1325–50, <a href="/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Art" title="National Gallery of Art">National Gallery of Art</a></li>
<li><i>The Archangel Gabriel</i>, <a href="/wiki/Masolino_da_Panicale" title="Masolino da Panicale">Masolino da Panicale</a>, c. 1420–30, <a href="/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Art" title="National Gallery of Art">National Gallery of Art</a></li>
<li><i>Justice between the Archangels Michael and Gabriel</i>, <a href="/wiki/Jacobello_del_Fiore" title="Jacobello del Fiore">Jacobello del Fiore</a>, 1421</li>
<li><i>Merode Altarpiece</i> (Triptych), <a href="/wiki/Robert_Campin" title="Robert Campin">Robert Campin</a>, c. 1425, <a href="/wiki/Metropolitan_Museum_of_Art" title="Metropolitan Museum of Art">Metropolitan Museum of Art</a></li>
<li><i>The Angel Gabriel</i>, <a href="/wiki/Agostino_di_Duccio" title="Agostino di Duccio">Agostino di Duccio</a>, c. 1450</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Annunciation_(Leonardo)" title="Annunciation (Leonardo)">Annunciation</a></i>, <a href="/wiki/Leonardo_da_Vinci" title="Leonardo da Vinci">Leonardo da Vinci</a>, c. 1475</li>
<li><i>The Angel Gabriel</i>, <a href="/wiki/Neroccio_d%27Landi" class="mw-redirect" title="Neroccio d&#39;Landi">Neroccio d'Landi</a>, c. 1490</li>
<li><i>The Angel Gabriel</i>, late 15th or early 16th century, Flemish, <a href="/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Art" title="National Gallery of Art">National Gallery of Art</a></li>
<li><i>The Angel Gabriel</i>, Ferrari Gaudenzio, 1511, <a href="/wiki/National_Gallery,_London" class="mw-redirect" title="National Gallery, London">National Gallery, London</a></li>
<li><i>Gabriel delivering the Annunciation</i> <a href="/wiki/El_Greco" title="El Greco">El Greco</a>, 1575 (pictured above)</li>
<li><i>Go Down Death</i>, <a href="/wiki/Aaron_Douglas_(artist)" title="Aaron Douglas (artist)">Aaron Douglas</a>, 1934</li></ul>
<p>The Military Order of Saint Gabriel was established to recognize "individuals who have made significant contributions to the U.S. Army Public Affairs community and practice." The medallion depicts St. Gabriel sounding a trumpet, while the obverse displays the Army Public Affairs emblem.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Television">Television</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabriel&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Television">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><i><a href="/wiki/The_Twilight_Zone_(1959_TV_series)" title="The Twilight Zone (1959 TV series)">The Twilight Zone</a></i> (1960) episode "<a href="/wiki/A_Passage_for_Trumpet" title="A Passage for Trumpet">A Passage for Trumpet</a>" – The down-and-out musician Joey Crown (<a href="/wiki/Jack_Klugman" title="Jack Klugman">Jack Klugman</a>) meets an enigmatic trumpet player named "Gabe" (played by <a href="/wiki/John_Anderson_(actor)" title="John Anderson (actor)">John Anderson</a>), in what has been described as <a href="/wiki/Rod_Serling" title="Rod Serling">Rod Serling</a>'s version of <i><a href="/wiki/It%27s_a_Wonderful_Life" title="It&#39;s a Wonderful Life">It's a Wonderful Life</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Supernatural_(U.S._TV_series)" class="mw-redirect" title="Supernatural (U.S. TV series)">Supernatural</a></i> (2005) – Gabriel, portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Richard_Speight_Jr." title="Richard Speight Jr.">Richard Speight Jr.</a>, is a runaway archangel who kills people he deems evil, also interacting with <a href="/wiki/List_of_angels_in_Supernatural" title="List of angels in Supernatural">other angels</a>, including his siblings Michael, Raphael, and Lucifer.</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Dominion_(TV_series)" title="Dominion (TV series)">Dominion</a></i> (2014) – Gabriel, portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Carl_Beukes" title="Carl Beukes">Carl Beukes</a>, is the series antagonist, who plans to kill the Archangel Michael and annihilate humanity.</li>
<li><i><a href="/wiki/Now_Apocalypse" title="Now Apocalypse">Now Apocalypse</a></i> (2019) – Gabriel, portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Tyler_Posey" title="Tyler Posey">Tyler Posey</a>, is an enigmatic trumpet player who has a passionate tryst with series protagonist Ulysses Zane before warning him about an impending apocalypse.</li>
<li>Amazon Prime miniseries <i><a href="/wiki/Good_Omens_(miniseries)" class="mw-redirect" title="Good Omens (miniseries)">Good Omens</a></i> (2019) – Gabriel is portrayed by <a href="/wiki/Jon_Hamm" title="Jon Hamm">Jon Hamm</a>. The show is based on <a href="/wiki/Good_Omens" title="Good Omens">the novel</a> by <a href="/wiki/Terry_Pratchett" title="Terry Pratchett">Terry Pratchett</a> and <a href="/wiki/Neil_Gaiman" title="Neil Gaiman">Neil Gaiman</a>.</li></ul>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="References">References</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabriel&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: References">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Gabriel&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist">
<div class="mw-references-wrap"><ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-1">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Including, but not limited to: <a href="/wiki/Yazidism" title="Yazidism">Yazidism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mormonism" title="Mormonism">Mormonism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rastafari" title="Rastafari">Rastafari</a>, <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A1bism" title="Bábism">Bábism</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith" title="Baháʼí Faith">Baháʼí Faith</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-5"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-5">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a href="/wiki/Hebrew_language" title="Hebrew language">Hebrew</a>: <span lang="he" dir="rtl">גַּבְרִיאֵל</span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Romanization_of_Hebrew" title="Romanization of Hebrew">romanized</a>:&#160;</small><span title="Hebrew-language romanization"><i lang="he-Latn">Gaḇrīʾēl</i></span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Literal_translation" title="Literal translation">lit.</a>&#8201;</small>&#39;El [God] is my man&#39;; <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Greek_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Ancient Greek language">Ancient Greek</a>: <span lang="grc">Γαβριήλ</span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Romanization_of_Ancient_Greek" class="mw-redirect" title="Romanization of Ancient Greek">romanized</a>:&#160;</small><span title="Ancient Greek-language romanization"><i lang="grc-Latn">Gabriḗl</i></span>; <a href="/wiki/Latin_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin language">Latin</a>: <i lang="la">Gabriel</i>; <a href="/wiki/Coptic_language" title="Coptic language">Coptic</a>: <span lang="cop">Ⲅⲁⲃⲣⲓⲏⲗ</span>, <small>romanized:&#160;</small><span title="Coptic-language romanization"><i lang="cop-Latn">Gabriêl</i></span>; <a href="/wiki/Amharic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Amharic language">Amharic</a>: <span lang="am">ገብርኤል</span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Romanization_of_Amharic" class="mw-redirect" title="Romanization of Amharic">romanized</a>:&#160;</small><span title="Amharic-language romanization"><i lang="am-Latn">Gabrəʾel</i></span>; <a href="/wiki/Imperial_Aramaic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Imperial Aramaic language">Imperial Aramaic</a>: <span lang="arc" dir="rtl">ܓ݁ܰܒ݂ܪܺܝܐܝܶܠ</span>, <small>romanized:&#160;</small><span title="Imperial Aramaic-language romanization"><i lang="arc-Latn">Gaḇrīʾēl</i></span>; <a href="/wiki/Arabic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Arabic language">Arabic</a>: <span lang="ar" dir="rtl">جِبْرِيل</span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic" title="Romanization of Arabic">romanized</a>:&#160;</small><span title="Arabic-language romanization"><i lang="ar-Latn">Jibrīl</i></span>, also <span title="Arabic-language text"><span lang="ar" dir="rtl">جبرائيل</span></span>, <span title="Arabic-language romanization"><i lang="ar-Latn">Jibrāʾīl</i></span> or <span title="Arabic-language romanization"><i lang="ar-Latn">Jabrāʾīl</i></span>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-67"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-67">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Found in Mustadrak al Sahihayn.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The complete narration from <a href="/wiki/Al-Hakim_al-Nishapuri" title="Al-Hakim al-Nishapuri">Al-Hakim al-Nishapuri</a> were:... <i>Abu Abdullah Muhammad bin Yaqoub has reported from Ibrahim bin Abdullah Al-Saadi,  who told us Muhammad bin Khalid bin Uthma, told us Musa bin Yaqoub, told me Abu Al-Huwairith, that Muhammad bin Jubayr bin Mut’im told him, that he heard Ali - may God be pleased with him - addresses the people, and he said: While I was leaving from the well of Badr, a strong wind came, the like of which I had never seen, then it left, then came a strong wind, the like of which I have never seen except for the one before it, then it went, then came a strong wind that I did not see before. I have never seen anything like it except for the one before it, and the first wind was Gabriel descended among a thousand angels with the Messenger of God - may God bless him and grant him peace - and the second wind was Michael who descended among a thousand angels to the right of the Messenger of God - may God bless him and his family and grant them peace - and Abu Bakr was On his right, and the third wind was Israfil. He descended with a thousand angels on the side of the Messenger of God - may God's prayers and peace be upon him and his family - and I was on the right side. When God Almighty defeated his enemies, the Messenger of God - may God's prayers and peace be upon him and his family - carried me on his horse, I blew up, and I fell On my heels, I prayed to God Almighty...</i> <a href="/w/index.php?title=Ibn_al_Mulqin&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ibn al Mulqin (page does not exist)">Ibn al Mulqin</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://id.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ibnu_Al-Mulaqqin" class="extiw" title="id:Ibnu Al-Mulaqqin">id</a>&#93;</span>, Hadith scholar from Cordoba of 13-14 AD century, evaluate this hadith that he found weaknesses in Musa ibn Yaqoub and Abu al Huwairith chain, so he deemed there is weakness about this hadith.<sup id="cite_ref-Al_Mulqin_Mukhtaras_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Al_Mulqin_Mukhtaras-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> However, recent scholarship from Ali Hasan al-Halabi has noted there is another hadith which supported the participation of Raphael in Badr<sup id="cite_ref-Israfil_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Israfil-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-69"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-69">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">According to Islamic belief in weak chain of Hadith, Raphael were acknowledged as angel who were tasked to blower of <a href="/wiki/Armageddon" title="Armageddon">Armageddon</a> trumpet, and one of archangels who bear the <a href="/wiki/Throne_of_God" title="Throne of God">Throne of God</a> on their back.<sup id="cite_ref-Israfil2_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Israfil2-68">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-71"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-71">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">According to one Hadith, Muhammad were told that the angels that appeared in the battle of Badr were highest in status and the "best of angels" according to Gabriel in Hadith narrated by Muhammad.<sup id="cite_ref-Lives_Of_The_Sahaba_39_–_Az-Zubayr_Ibn_Al-Awwam_–_PT_01_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Lives_Of_The_Sahaba_39_–_Az-Zubayr_Ibn_Al-Awwam_–_PT_01-70">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-76"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-76">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">According to one narration, during the battle, Muhammad has found an angel whom he though as Zubayr standing next to him, which then prompted Muhammad to command him to attack, which the angel, in Zubayr appearance, simply replied, "I am not Zubayr." Thus, according to Hadith expert this another indication that the angels truly came down with the appearance of Zubayr during Badr.<sup id="cite_ref-Kisah_Teladan_20_Shahabat_Nabi_untuk_Anak_75-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kisah_Teladan_20_Shahabat_Nabi_untuk_Anak-75">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>

<li id="cite_note-19"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-19">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://archive.today/20120720085450/http://www.avbtab.org/rc/read/dedicate.htm">THE Dedication (Jesus' birth)</a> "The priests serve 4 weeks per year: 1 week twice a year in courses, and the two week-long feasts, unleavened bread and tabernacles. Pentecost is a one-day observance, which would have come before Zacharias' (the 8th) course began, or at the latest, the 1st day of his course, which was from 12 thru 18 Sivan, or noon on the 19th, if Josephus is correct that courses changed at noon on the sabbaths." Josephus Antiquities b.7 ch.14 s.7 "eight days, from sabbath to sabbath." Josephus against Apion b.2 sect.8 "mid-day"</span>

<li id="cite_note-svm-37"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-svm_37-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-svm_37-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-svm_37-2"><sup><i><b>c</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text">S. Vernon McCasland, "Gabriel's Trumpet", <i>Journal of Bible and Religion</i> <b>9</b>:3:159–161 (August 1941) <a href="/wiki/JSTOR_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="JSTOR (identifier)">JSTOR</a>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/stable/1456405">1456405</a></span>

<li id="cite_note-38"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-38">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In Judaism, trumpets are prominent, and they seem to be blown by God himself, or sometimes <a href="/wiki/Michael_(archangel)" title="Michael (archangel)">Michael</a>. In Islamic tradition, it is <a href="/wiki/Israfil" title="Israfil">Israfil</a> who blows the trumpet, though he is not named in the <a href="/wiki/Qur%27an" class="mw-redirect" title="Qur&#39;an">Qur'an</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-39"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-39">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20210822213239/http://forums.orthodoxchristianity.net/attachments/the-peace-hour-pdf.18766/">"Peace Hour (After Sunset)"</a>. <i>orthodoxchristianity.net</i>. Archived from <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://forums.orthodoxchristianity.net/attachments/the-peace-hour-pdf.18766/">the original</a> on 22 August 2021<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">22 August</span> 2021</span>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=orthodoxchristianity.net&amp;rft.atitle=Peace+Hour+%28After+Sunset%29&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fforums.orthodoxchristianity.net%2Fattachments%2Fthe-peace-hour-pdf.18766%2F&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AGabriel" class="Z3988"></span></span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-40"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-40">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Walters MS 543, fol. 14.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-41"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-41">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The widespread understanding of Gabriel's horn as a symbol of the end of time in U.S. Southern culture, is apparent from its appearance in the University of Texas's school spirit song, "<a href="/wiki/The_Eyes_of_Texas" title="The Eyes of Texas">The Eyes of Texas</a>" (1903): <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://texassports.com/sports/2013/7/28/traditions_0728131333.aspx?id=267">"The eyes of Texas are upon you, until Gabriel blows his horn."</a> Likewise in <a href="/wiki/Marc_Connelly" title="Marc Connelly">Marc Connelly</a>'s play based on negro spirituals, <i><a href="/wiki/The_Green_Pastures" title="The Green Pastures">The Green Pastures</a></i> (1930), Gabriel has his beloved trumpet constantly with him, and the Lord has to warn him not to blow it too soon.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-42"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-42">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Milton, <i>Paradise Lost</i>, XI.72ff</span>
</li>

"  [من فوائد جلسة مع طلبة العلم /16/ذو الحجة/1432 ] __________________ " ... فهل يحسن بنا وقد أنضينا قرائحنا في تعلم هذه السنة المطهرة، وبذلنا في العمل بها جهد المستطيع، وركبنا المخاطر في الدعوة إليها؛ هل يحسن بنا بعد هذا كله أن نسكت لهؤلاء عن هذه الدعوى الباطلة، ونوليهم منا ما تولوا ونبلعهم ريقهم، وهل يحسن بنا أن لا يكون لنا في الدفاع عنها ما كان منا في الدعوة إليها؟ إنا إذن لمقصرون!..."<span class="cs1-kern-right"></span></q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=Muslim.or.id&amp;rft.atitle=Apakah+Malaikat+Israfil+Bertugas+Meniup+Sangkakala+pada+Hari+Kiamat%3F+%281%29&amp;rft.date=2015&amp;rft.aulast=Hakim&amp;rft.aufirst=Saifuddin&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fmuslim.or.id%2F24549-apakah-malaikat-israfil-bertugas-meniup-sangkakala-pada-hari-kiamat-1.html&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AGabriel" class="Z3988"></span></span>
